SOCi Launches MCP Server and Expands Integrations, Strengthening the Context Layer Behind Its Agentic Workforce

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

With more than 140 integrations, from Snowflake and Salesforce to Olo, Semrush and Canva, SOCi's agentic workforce learns from, works with and extends beyond the SOCi platform

SAN DIEGO, Nov. 19, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- SOCi Inc., the leader in agentic workforce solutions for multi-location enterprises, today announced the general availability of its Model Context Protocol (MCP) server, along with an expansion of the integrations that feed its context layer. SOCi's context layer is the unified source of truth for brand knowledge, guidelines and goals that guides SOCi's agentic workforce of more than 400,000 deployed Genius Agentsâ¢. With the MCP and more than 140 integrations, that intelligence now flows in from the systems enterprises already run on and out to the places their teams already work.

The announcement moves SOCi from a platform that agents work in, to an interconnected intelligence layer that speaks to, learns from and works with a brand's most important systems.

"Agents will only ever be as smart as the context they are allowed to see," said Afif Khoury, Founder and CEO of SOCi. "SOCi's context layer already knows the brand, every location and what earns visibility in local discovery. Our integrations bring in the rest of the stack, and through the MCP, our agents and tools are available wherever our customers' teams already work."

Context in: an agentic workforce that learns from the whole stack

SOCi's Genius Agents make better decisions when they can see more of the business. SOCi's integrations bring signals from the systems that run enterprise operations into the context layer, so agents act on what is actually happening at each location. Alongside SOCi's premier integrations with Google, Meta, Yelp, Apple and Bing, SOCi connects to:

Context out: SOCi's intelligence wherever work happens

SOCi's MCP server, now generally available to all SOCi customers, lets AI assistants and agent frameworks that support the Model Context Protocol call SOCi's standard platform tools, agentic tools, and reporting and insights directly, so the results become part of the context those assistants work from. A marketer can ask their AI assistant to "show me my SOCi locations" or "which locations need review responses," and the assistant calls SOCi, gets the answer and responds, without the marketer logging in to another interface. SOCi customers can also work with the same tools through Ask Genius, SOCi's agentic front door to the SOCi platform.

Access is governed by SOCi's enterprise permissions. SOCi Shield applies brand and compliance rules with approval paths for work that needs a person's judgment, and SOCi holds SOC 2 Type II and ISO/IEC 42001 credentials.

"Context flows in, so agents are grounded in how the business actually runs," said Alo Sarv, co-founder and CTO of SOCi. "And through the MCP, SOCi's tools, agents and insights flow out to wherever our customers' teams are already working. That is what makes the workforce compound."

From software you manage to work that gets done

Enterprise brands no longer have to choose between a platform that works and a stack that connects. SOCi's agents execute local search, social and reputation work at every location, and the context layer behind them is grounded in more of the business with every system it connects to.

To learn more about SOCi's integrations and MCP server, visit www.soci.ai.

SOCi is redefining how multi-location enterprises achieve local and AI search visibility with the world's first agentic workforce. Built specifically for distributed brands, SOCi's brand-trained agents are guided by a unified visibility engine that autonomously executes and optimizes local marketing work across AI search, GEO ecosystems, social, and reviews, ensuring every location is visible, discoverable, and growing in today's localized digital landscape. Trusted by leading brands like Ford, Ace Hardware, and Liberty Tax, and recognized by Fast Company as one of the World's Most Innovative Companies, SOCi transforms marketing from software teams manage into autonomous work executed intelligently, consistently, and at enterprise scale. Learn more at www.soci.ai or contact [email protected].

Repeated Epic Universe visits is about to be a lot easier.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Universal Epic Universe is currently celebrating its second holiday season with more holiday decorations than last year and a new Dark Universe ride currently under construction. There’s even more gifts from the theme park during this time of merriment! It’s just been announced that for the first time ever one can obtain an annual pass. The news has lead to a mix of huge excitement and tons of questions.

In a Universal Parks press release, the Orlando resort has shared that annual passes featuring Universal Epic Universe access are coming in early December. Guests with current annual passes will be able to upgrade their current passes, and those looking to purchase one can do so at this time. Unlike the other parks, there will be a reservation system in place with the earliest date to go with a pass being January 4, 2027. Check out some of the spirited reactions from Twitter:

Ever since Epic Universe opened its gates in May of last year, it has quickly become a favorite park with tons of great rides to check out. For those who want to return there again and again (and perhaps make time to notice the park’s fun easter eggs) an annual pass is always a great value. Fans are rightfully hyped.

Whenever there’s a big change at one of Universal Orlando’s parks, inquiries typically follow, and there’s a lot popping up amidst the announcement. Here’s a few big ones:

Universal was all over the comment section trying to answer some of these questions. Most of them seem to point to there being another wave of details being on the way, but for now Universal Orlando’s Annual Pass website has been refreshed with current steps to take.

Universal Orlando Resort will still offer four different types of pass tiers as it currently does, but the announcement includes a complete re-branding of the system. Those who hold these memberships will be called “Pass Members” rather than “Passholders” and the type names will go from being called Seasonal, Power, Preferred and Premier to Bronze, Silver, Gold and Platinum. They’re also going completely digital with all physical passes being phased out over time.

In regards to the Epic Universe upgrade, current Passholders will be given a timeframe where they can upgrade their passes first before anyone else. Those who are in their renewal period will also have the option to “pay a price difference of $125 to $410 to renew into a Pass with Universal Epic Universe access, depending on Pass type.” Annual passes currently cost between $475 and $1015, based on that price difference info, the price range might have changed to between $600 to $1425, but all the details are not yet clear to anyone quite yet.

Universal is already teasing more information on the way “soon,”. Either way, it’s quite exciting that those interested will soon get to enjoy Epic Universe over and over.

‘GTA VI’ Sales Surge Over $2B On Launch Day As Vice City-Mania Takes Over

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Executive Editor, Legal, Labor & Politics

The long awaited and twice delayed launch of Grand Theft Auto VI has made a killing and taken over the zeitgeist.

By killing, we don’t just mean the mayhem of Vice City. We mean cold hard cash, digitally

Less than 24 hours after the fictional Florida-set video game went live at midnight today, GTA VI has made over $2.36 billion, according to Sensor Tower. “GTA VI has captivated consumers, becoming 2026’s best-selling game based on pre-orders alone, with total pre-order revenue exceeding $2 billion through launch day,” the market intelligence company’s SVP of Video Game Insights, Karl Kontus told Deadline Thursday. Related Stories Video Games 'Abbott Elementary's Janelle James Says She "Bullied" Her Way To 'GTA 6': "I Pursued, Conceptualized, Pitched, Scripted & Voice Acted" News Judge Weighs Whether To Allow Kennedy Center To Proceed With Closure Plan

In fact, with last night’s fully GTA VI themed Miami Heat‘s primetime 137-130 win over the Milwaukee Bucks as but one example of the vast marketing machine at play, sales surged in the final hours before launch as fans rushed to download the 212GB game. Watch on Deadline

We had a (Good)time last night! 🤩 pic.twitter.com/AsMHOch4qs

Cementing the latest Rockstar Games‘ offering as the most successful entertainment industry launch ever, the decade and more in the making GTA VI saw sales rise $710 million since early on November 18 when pre-sales sat at an already record breaking $1.65 billion. Currently, the download only GTA VI has sold 24.59 million units, with 75% of them on Playstation and the remainder on Xbox consoles.

At this rate, with millions around the globe seemingly taking an adult snow day or PTO to play the first new GTA game in 13 years, VI is on track to reach further financial heights of $3 billion within weeks. That figure could be dwarfed when the single player GTA VI lands on the multi-player and lucrative GTA Online platform in the next several months.

As large as that number will go, leaving far far behind, the previous launch day number #1 of the $800 million GTA V earned in 2013, the (mis)adventures of crooks on the rise Jason and Lucia in the corrupt and free-wheeling humid state of Leonida is having an almost equal impact on the culture.

A throwback in more ways than one to the long lost days of American monoculture, the eighth full game in the GTA franchise has conquered the conversation online and offline today.

Estimates of economic losses due to absentee workers Thursday may be offset by GTA VI profits, but nothing could stop a full partisan upbraiding from Fox News. “If this generation wants to preach about affordability, but they’re willing to cost billions of dollars in revenue loss on a day like today to go play a game, that probably tells you everything you need to know about what we need to be talking with this younger generation about,” the Murdoch-owned cabler newser’s Harris Faulkner declared this AM. “Get yourself to work.”

Of course, taking into account the controversy that the ultra violent GTA franchise has courted since its 1997 debut, such a berating falls nicely into the best tradition of the P.T. Barnum’s supposed quip of “there is no such thing as bad publicity.” Along with Fox, CNN, NewsNation, the BBC, the New York Times, the Guardian and the upscale New Yorker have all run a number of segments and features on VI today. On YouTube and Twitch, hundreds of live feeds are up busting internet viewership with millions watching people playing the over 150 hour long game or detailing hacks, easter eggs and just cruising around.

To that, GTA VI has also been widely name dropped and appropriated today by an unlikely collection of bandwagon jumpers.

The White House, HGTV, a sycophantic Vice-President JD Vance, Miami International Airport, Matt Johnson’s The The (who legitimately has a tune once again on one of the many in-game radio stations), Spotify, the Interior Department, and the UK’s Foreign, Commonwealth & Development Office, have all been hooking themselves to GTA VI in some witty, some cringe inducing and some just meh posts.

Anthropic rival says companies can (mostly) skip the pricey models

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

On the same day that Anthropic went public in the largest IPO on record, with investor enthusiasm for the AI lab through the roof, far away at the Slush startup conference in Helsinki, the topic du jour was open-source everything.

That’s the enormous opportunity being chased by companies like Fireworks AI, as enterprises seek ways to avoid paying frontier prices for their AI needs.

Fireworks runs AI models for other businesses. It specializes in open-weight models, which anyone can download, and helps customers tune them on their own data so they run faster and cheaper than the models of Anthropic, OpenAI and other heavyweights and are harder for rivals to copy. Customers have included Uber, Shopify and Notion. Co-founder Chenyu Zhao and I spoke on stage toward the end of the day.

Zhao and many others believe companies are gravitating toward platforms like his because frontier models are pricey and, in most cases, he says, unnecessary unless you need the very latest and greatest generalized intelligence. There are also growing concerns that frontier labs absorb too much of their customers’ know-how, even if only through the prompts used to get answers, a worry Microsoft CEO Satya Nadella raised in July.

Fireworks has also gotten its first black eye, having disclosed two security incidents this fall. In one, hackers reached API keys and tokens stored as secrets for a limited number of accounts; in the other, attackers breached its cloud provider, made their way into a development system, and nabbed customer data, including billing records and some datasets.

Notion says AI chat transcripts from fewer than 1% of its workspaces were exposed, but it has disconnected from Fireworks. Uber says hackers stole the names, addresses, birth dates and driver’s license numbers of about 150,000 drivers through a vendor that TechCrunch identified as Fireworks. Fireworks will have to win back the confidence of customers like them.

Fireworks, assigned a $17.5 billion valuation by its investors in summer, is reportedly in “early talks” for yet another round, though Zhao declined to comment.

There’s money chasing everyone in the category, seems like. Rival companies Together AI and Baseten have announced massive rounds in recent months. On Wednesday, Palo Alto Networks said it will buy Applied Compute, which, like Fireworks, helps companies train and run custom AI models on their own data. The deal for the startup, founded just a year ago by former OpenAI researchers, is $4.5 billion, all of it in stock.

Here’s some of what Zhao had to say. This conversation has been edited for length and clarity.

You spent a decade at Google, including managing a 50-person engineering team on Google Cloud Vertex AI. Why leave when you did?

When you meet great co-founders, it’s obvious within five minutes that they’re really smart. I was sold after talking to Lin [Qiao] and Dima [Dzhulgakov]. It’s also important to find people who are genuinely nice, people you’ll enjoy working with.

How do you describe Fireworks to a CEO who has never heard of it?

I describe Fireworks as a specialized intelligence platform. OpenAI and Anthropic are building general intelligence. We use open models, which are often smaller, cheaper and faster, and let companies add their own data to specialize them. That way you own your AI rather than being beholden to giant AI conglomerates. If you’re just using Opus or Sonnet, that’s not a differentiator.

How far behind are open models right now?

A few years ago I’d have said a year, a year and a half. Then came DeepSeek and a waterfall of fantastic open models. These days it’s at most six months behind, probably closer to three.

If the models are so close behind, why are businesses slow to switch?

Frontier models are a household brand, and before you find product-market fit, using the best model is low risk. But once you pass 10,000 or 100,000 users and you’re spending $10,000 to $100,000 a month on tokens, it’s time to think about open source.

In July, Microsoft CEO Satya Nadella warned that companies using proprietary AI models pay twice, once in money and again in know-how. Do you think that warning had a material effect?

We think we’ll live in a two-model world, with frontier and open models in parallel. You want the best intelligence for high-level planning, but you don’t need a frontier model for basic arithmetic. That’s why there’s so much interest in routing, and why people now think about cost per task, not just cost per token.

Anthropic went public today. What do you think of its valuation? There’s one scenario in which it just continues to cement its place in this sort of oligopoly; there’s another where it starts to lose traction.

On the record: I’ll probably call my broker tonight and buy a few shares. This is a long-term bet. In 10 years AI usage will be at a completely different scale, and that takes an incredible amount of capital, which is why these companies are going public.

Fireworks has had a couple of security breaches recently. What do you tell customers?

Every company is probably seeing an unprecedented frequency of attacks. For our incident, a lot of the motivation was LLM jacking: hunting through providers for API keys and secrets to sell tokens on illicit marketplaces. Our promise is zero data retention for inference traffic, and there’s no indication that has been compromised.

Notion has disconnected from Fireworks. Did you lose other customers?

I don’t think I can publicly name the specific customers that were impacted. Some, such as Notion and Cursor, have gone public already. It was a small subset of our customers that had this data impact.

How did that customer data get exposed, given your zero-retention promise?

Notion was using batch prediction, where you upload a dataset that’s stored at rest, so it’s not a zero-retention product by nature. We’re moving those services to an online flow so we keep data only as long as we need it.

Jensen Huang left Nvidia last week, and Nvidia has backed Fireworks since 2024. Does that affect you?

It’s been a blow to the industry, and we feel for Jensen and his family. But Nvidia has always been a strong supporter of open source, and I don’t see that changing. We expect it to keep investing in Nemotron, and we’re excited to keep partnering with them.

Anyone who's watched an episode of The Bear or a YouTube cooking show immediately recognized a central supporting character in GTA 6, Jason's lovable, rambling redneck friend, Cal. He's obviously Matty Matheson, the chef and actor best-known as Neil Fak on the FX TV show The Bear. And yet, he definitely isn't, because Matheson himself has confirmed he isn't in GTA 6 at all.

Matheson clearly caught wind of the comparisons he's been getting since Cal was revealed in trailers and promotional images a couple of years back, and took to Instagram to dispel the suspicions. "I just wanted to say that I'm not in Grand Theft Auto 6," he says as an image of Cal appears on-screen. "I'm not. That's not me, it's not my voice. But uh, yeah, I don't know... So, it's not me. Have a beautiful day."

Cal is quickly becoming one of my favorite characters in GTA 6. He's the lovable oaf that every protagonist needs by their side in a rollicking, dangerous adventure like the one Jason and Lucia get into in GTA 6. I say that as a protective preface before the following sentence: I refuse to believe that isn't Matty Matheson. I mean, it definitely isn't his voice or performance, but Cal, in spirit, will always be a digitized Matty Matheson to me, or at least the exaggerated, high-energy persona he assumes in a lot of his videos. Or maybe he's Danny McBride.

Joking aside, it's actually former Saturday Night Live mainstay Bobby Moynihan that plays Cal in GTA 6, according to Vulture, and you know what, that's damn good fit I hadn't even considered before researching for this story.

Stowers Institute appoints Brandon Mayer as Chief Technology Officer

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The veteran technology executive brings more than two decades of experience in digital transformation and enterprise technology, including recent work in artificial intelligence.

KANSAS CITY, Mo., Nov. 19, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- The Stowers Institute for Medical Research has appointed Brandon Mayer as Vice President and Chief Technology Officer. Mayer brings more than two decades of experience leading technology strategy and large-scale organizational transformation.

Mayer most recently served as Chief Information Officer and Chief Transformation Officer at Employers Reassurance Corporation (ERAC), a GE Aerospace company. His expertise spans cloud infrastructure, cybersecurity, data analytics, and artificial intelligence.

In his new role, Mayer will lead the Stowers Institute's information technology strategy, research and advanced computing infrastructure, enterprise systems, cybersecurity, and digital transformation initiatives. He will work closely with both scientific leadership and leadership in administrative scientific support. Mayer will report to Kristin Bechard, Stowers Chief Financial Officer and Treasurer, to strengthen the technological capabilities that support foundational biological research and enable scientists to manage and analyze increasingly complex data.

"Brandon brings a valuable combination of strategic vision, technical expertise, and experience leading transformation within complex global organizations," said Bechard. "As the opportunities of our scientific research continue to evolve, so must the systems and infrastructure that support it. Brandon's leadership will be instrumental in ensuring our technology investments are thoughtful, secure, and positioned to meet the Institute's needs for years to come."

At ERAC, Mayer led a technology and transformation organization and was responsible for overseeing enterprise applications, cloud infrastructure, cybersecurity, data, artificial intelligence platforms and engineering, as well as strategic technology partnerships.

"The Stowers Institute is an extraordinary organization with a mission that places discovery and innovation at its center," said Mayer. "Technology has an increasingly important role to play in enabling scientific progress, from the infrastructure that supports foundational research to the responsible application of AI and advanced data analytics."

During his tenure, Mayer led ERAC's transition from shared corporate technology systems to an independent, cloud-based environment. He also advanced initiatives involving Microsoft Fabric, enterprise analytics, and generative AI, while helping establish governance practices for the responsible use of emerging technologies.

The Institute's distinctive funding model reflects the intent of founders Jim and Virginia Stowers to advance biomedical research for the benefit of human health. Every year, 40% of dividends from American Century Investments support biomedical research at the Stowers Institute, directing more than $2 billion since 2000. This sustained investment enables the Stowers scientists to pursue fundamental biological questions with the potential to inform new approaches to understanding, treating, and preventing disease.

As scientific research generates increasingly large and complex datasets, advances in computational technologies, artificial intelligence, and secure research infrastructure are creating new opportunities to investigate fundamental biological processes.

Mayer added, "I look forward to working with scientists and colleagues across the Institute to build capabilities that support their research while preparing us for the many opportunities ahead."

About the Stowers Institute for Medical Research

Founded in 1994 through the generosity of Jim Stowers, founder of American Century Investments, and his wife, Virginia, the Stowers Institute for Medical Research is a nonprofit biomedical research organization with a focus on foundational research. Its mission is to expand our understanding of the secrets of life and improve life's quality through innovative approaches to the causes, treatment, and prevention of diseases.

The Institute consists of 20 independent research programs. Of the approximately 500 members, more than 370 are scientific staff, including principal investigators, technology center directors, postdoctoral scientists, graduate students, and technical support staff. Learn more at www.stowers.org and www.stowers.org/gradschool.

Experimental compound burns fat without sacrificing muscle

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A new approach to weight loss could help the body burn more fat while preserving muscle, potentially overcoming one of the major drawbacks of existing obesity treatments. Researchers at the University of California, Berkeley, have identified a compound that increases the amount of energy the body uses rather than simply reducing how much food a person eats.

In experiments with mice, the compound improved blood sugar regulation, reduced harmful blood fats, and helped obese animals lose fat without a significant reduction in lean muscle mass. It also increased energy expenditure by up to 18%, even though the mice did not become more physically active or experience an increase in body temperature.

The findings, published in Science Advances, suggest a promising new direction for treating obesity, diabetes, and fatty liver disease. The experimental compound also appeared to enhance the benefits of popular weight-loss medications such as Ozempic and Wegovy when the treatments were used together. However, its effectiveness and safety have not yet been established in humans.

A Different Approach to Weight Loss Than Ozempic

Over the past five years, medications known as GLP-1 drugs have transformed the treatment of obesity and other metabolic conditions, including diabetes and fatty liver disease. Drugs marketed as Ozempic, Wegovy, Mounjaro, and Zepbound have helped many patients achieve substantial weight loss while improving their ability to regulate blood sugar.

Despite these benefits, the medications have important limitations. Nausea and other digestive problems can make treatment difficult for some patients. Because these drugs reduce appetite and food consumption, they can also increase the risk of inadequate nutrition and loss of muscle mass. Over time, losing too much muscle can contribute to weakness, frailty, and other health complications.

Berkeley researchers wanted to investigate a fundamentally different strategy. Rather than focusing on reducing calorie intake, they explored whether it would be possible to increase the amount of energy the body burns.

This process, known as energy expenditure, includes the calories used to maintain basic bodily functions, regulate metabolism, and support everyday activities. Increasing energy expenditure could potentially help people lose weight without relying exclusively on eating less.

"Body weight responds to two levers: taking in fewer calories, or spending more energy," said Anders Näär, a professor of metabolic biology and nutrition at UC Berkeley and senior author of the study. "GLP-1s work almost entirely on the first, so we went after the second."

A Compound That Burns Fat While Preserving Muscle

The researchers focused on a molecule called 5-tetradecyloxy-2-furoic acid (TOFA), which has an unusual combination of effects on the body's metabolism.

TOFA interferes with the production of lipids, a broad category of fatty substances that includes cholesterol and triglycerides. These molecules serve important functions, but excessive levels of certain lipids can contribute to metabolic disorders and cardiovascular disease.

At the same time, TOFA activates genes involved in taking up and burning fat for energy. This combination allows the compound to influence both how the body produces fat and how it uses fat as fuel.

In mice, treatment with TOFA produced several encouraging results. The animals became more sensitive to insulin, the hormone that helps move glucose from the bloodstream into cells. Improved insulin sensitivity makes it easier for the body to control blood sugar, an important benefit for people with diabetes or insulin resistance.

The compound also lowered triglyceride levels and improved characteristics of fatty liver disease, a condition in which excess fat accumulates inside liver cells and can eventually interfere with liver function.

Perhaps most notably, obese mice treated with TOFA lost weight primarily through reductions in body fat, without experiencing significant losses of lean muscle mass. This distinction could be especially valuable because maintaining muscle is important for strength, mobility, and long-term metabolic health.

Although TOFA was originally discovered in the 1970s, the new research uncovered additional properties that could make it particularly useful for treating metabolic disease.

The compound belongs to a group of substances known as ACC inhibitors. These compounds interfere with enzymes that help the body manufacture fatty acids, effectively slowing the production of certain fats.

Other ACC inhibitors have previously advanced to mid-stage clinical trials, but none has been approved to treat metabolic diseases. One major obstacle has been an unexpected side effect: many of these compounds increase triglycerides in the blood, potentially raising the risk of heart problems.

The Berkeley team discovered that TOFA does more than inhibit ACC. It also activates two cellular receptors called PPARα and PPARδ, which help regulate the genes responsible for fat metabolism.

These receptors function as molecular switches. When activated, they stimulate processes that allow cells to absorb fatty acids and break them down to produce energy. In effect, TOFA simultaneously reduces the production of certain fats and encourages the body to burn fat that is already available.

In mice, this activity increased energy expenditure by as much as 18%, without requiring additional physical activity or raising body temperature. Unlike other ACC inhibitors, TOFA also did not cause triglyceride levels to rise, potentially because of its additional effects on fat metabolism.

"TOFA appears to engage a coordinated metabolic response," said study first author Justin Y. Lee, a postdoctoral student at UCSF who conducted the research as a Ph.D. student at Berkeley. "It is not simply blocking lipid synthesis. It is also activating energy expenditure pathways that may help the body handle excess lipid and glucose more effectively."

To understand whether these effects could be reproduced with separate treatments, the scientists gave mice a combination of two different compounds. One blocked lipid production, while the other stimulated energy expenditure.

Surprisingly, this two-compound approach was less effective at improving overall metabolic health than TOFA alone. The finding suggests that TOFA's ability to influence multiple metabolic processes through a single molecule may be an important part of its effectiveness.

Could TOFA Make Ozempic and Wegovy More Effective?

The researchers also investigated whether TOFA could improve the results of existing GLP-1 medications.

They tested the compound alongside semaglutide, the active ingredient in Ozempic and Wegovy, as well as tirzepatide, which is marketed as Mounjaro and Zepbound.

These medications primarily help people lose weight by reducing appetite and food intake. TOFA, by contrast, targets the other side of the energy balance equation by increasing how much energy the body expends.

When the researchers combined TOFA with these medications in mice, the results were more favorable than those produced by either treatment alone. The combinations led to greater improvements in body weight, blood glucose regulation, insulin levels, and triglycerides.

This suggests that treatments targeting calorie intake and energy expenditure could potentially work together to produce stronger metabolic benefits.

"In our combination experiments, TOFA worked additively or synergistically with the GLP-1 appetite-suppressing drugs, so we view it as complementary rather than as a replacement," Näär said.

In other words, the researchers do not necessarily envision TOFA replacing medications such as Ozempic or Wegovy. Instead, it could eventually become an additional treatment that helps address obesity and related metabolic conditions through a different biological mechanism.

A Promising Discovery, but Human Trials Are Still Needed

Despite the encouraging results, the researchers emphasize that TOFA remains an experimental compound. All the findings so far come from animal studies, and scientists have not yet established whether it is safe or effective in humans.

Further research will be necessary to determine whether the same metabolic improvements can be achieved in people and whether the treatment could offer meaningful advantages over existing medications.

To help move the research toward potential clinical applications, the Berkeley scientists have established a biotechnology company called ReRx Therapeutics. The effort has received support from the university's life sciences entrepreneurship network, including Nucleate and Berkeley SkyDeck.

If the findings eventually translate to humans, TOFA could represent an important addition to the growing range of treatments for obesity and diabetes. By encouraging the body to use more energy while helping preserve muscle, the compound points toward a potentially different way to achieve healthier weight loss.

The research was funded through discretionary resources provided by UC Berkeley, with additional support from the UCSF Liver Center and the University of Michigan Animal Phenotyping Core.

Additional authors include Chi Zhu, Melissa A. Boldridge, Rachelle L. Stark, Lei Xu, Federico Gonzalez, Xin Tang, Kaitlyn T. Dang and Kook Son of Berkeley; Gracia Bonilla, Kashish Chetal and Ruslan I. Sadreyev of Massachusetts General Hospital; Kosuke Watari and Michael Karin of the University of California, San Diego; Christina Papa and Bilal N. Sheikh of the Helmholtz Center Munich; Prabha Ibrahim of ReRx Therapeutics.

Materials provided by University of California - Berkeley. Note: Content may be edited for style and length.

'I tell my son it's just fireworks': People inside besieged Yemeni city speak to BBC

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Houthi attacks on Taiz have killed more than 140 civilians since August, local government officials say

In the Yemeni city of Taiz, ringed by jagged mountains and Houthi rebel fighters, Ashwaq has only one way to try to soothe her frightened child - lie.

"When my 11-year-old son hears a loud sound, I tell him, 'Don't be afraid, it's just fireworks'," she says. "I don't want him to panic, but deep down I know he is old enough to understand what is happening around him. He has lived through war for most of his life."

Taiz has long been a prize - and a front line - in Yemen's civil war.

Houthi forces tightened their grip last month, fully encircling the government-controlled city, and the population - estimated to be between 600,000 and 1 million.

Since then, Yemen's third-largest city has been cut off from the world, and its residents have been largely unheard. We reached seven people inside the city who mapped out the anatomy of the siege.

They gave detailed accounts of the Houthis' death grip on Taiz. Only a minimal amount of food can get in. Prices for staples like rice, sugar and cooking oil have doubled or even trebled. Essential supplies, like medicine, water and fuel, are dwindling.

Burning piles of rubbish are producing toxic smoke that hangs over the city

On streets that used to hum with traffic and background chatter, a silence has fallen. Schools are closed and restaurants are shuttered. Suffering is being heaped on suffering. Piles of rubbish are trapped inside the city, alongside the people. When residents try to get rid of it by burning it, there is a toxic smoke. Even the air is now a Houthi weapon.

From positions on the hills, rebel fighters - and snipers - can wage war on the city below.

Estimates for the death toll vary widely. At the Office for Siege Affairs, a local government department, they say 148 civilians have been killed by Houthi attacks since August.

Some of those we contacted in Taiz do not want to be identified because they fear for their safety. Many in the city are reluctant to even speak on the phone because the Houthis control key mobile carriers.

"Sometimes it feels like the city has become a huge prison," says Ashwaq, a teacher and mother of three, who only wanted her first name to be used. She yearns for the old Taiz.

"It used to be a lively city," she says. "People loved life, worked hard, visited one another, and had hopes for the future, despite all the difficulties. Now, we hardly go out anymore. The [Houthi] shelling can happen any time, and sniper fire near the front lines makes even a short trip dangerous."

She keeps her son and two elder daughters indoors.

"I worry about them every single day, every hour, and honestly every minute," she says.

Taiz has been a major battleground in Yemen's civil war for more than a decade

War is not new to Taiz. The strategic city is the gateway to Aden - the temporary seat of the internationally recognised government - and to southern Yemen.

Houthi rebels - backed by Iran - reached the outskirts of Taiz in 2015, cutting off roads, and laying siege from three sides. A year earlier, they had seized Yemen's capital, Sanaa.

More than a decade on, the government, which is weak and fractured, has been unable to reclaim the capital and much of north-western Yemen. That's despite the backing of Saudi Arabia, and its air strikes.

In recent months, the conflict has flared up and expanded after a four-year lull. The Houthis made a lightning advance, taking territory from the government, and striking Saudi Arabia with missiles and drones.

A long-running civil war has become a threat to regional stability.

An emergency room doctor, whom we are calling Yaser, bears witness to that.

Among the war wounded recently brought to his hospital were four children, shot by Houthi snipers. They usually aim for the head and chest, he tells us. Two of the four children died.

The hardest thing he faces, as a father himself, is treating wounded children. "I have to rise to the responsibility, compose myself and support the families," he says. "It's a difficult situation that I cannot even put into words, when I see a father looking at his badly wounded child right in front of him."

Most of the injured are victims of shelling. Reaching the hospital can be an ordeal. Some families have to walk for hours from outlying areas.

They arrive exhausted, like the doctors who will treat them, and who are struggling with "everything", says Yaser. Even sterilising fluid for surgical instruments is in short supply.

He tells us the hospital is running on empty, and can carry on for two more weeks, "maybe".

Taiz's hospitals have lost 70% of their capacity and 120 health centres have closed, its governor says

If the emergency rooms are busy, so are the graveyards.

"Funerals heading to the cemeteries have become the defining scene in Taiz, around the clock," says Taiz's Governor, Nabil Shamsan.

It's not just bombs and bullets that kill, he tells us.

"The people of Taiz are losing their lives due to supply shortages, lack of food, and loss of income," he says. "Every single day, we bury dozens of Taiz residents."

He remains inside the city, and inside the siege. "I live among my people," he says.

On a video call, he looks weary but delivers a lengthy chronicle of a city being starved of oxygen - literally.

He tells us that hospitals have lost 70% of their capacity, and 120 health centres have closed. Diseases are spreading, neonatal deaths have increased, and malnutrition is surging. Hundreds of kidney patients are not getting dialysis because they can't get to hospitals or because fuel is short when they do.

The Houthi leader, Abdul Malik al-Houthi, has denied Taiz is besieged. Late last month, he claimed that routes remained open for food, medicine and humanitarian aid.

That is denied by the government, residents of Taiz, aid agencies and the United Nations.

What's at stake in the battle for the city is more than territory, says Shamsan.

He says the Houthis - who mostly follow a branch of Shia Islam - want to change the character of this predominantly Sunni city, long known as a seat of learning and a bastion of resistance.

"Everyone knows that Taiz is a hub of culture, a city characterised by freedom, political pluralism and all forms of civic life," he says. In sharp contrast, he says, the Houthis aim to impose their strict religious doctrine and drag the city "back to the dark ages".

Whatever the pressure on the city and its people, the governor insists the Houthis will never win.

"Until the population perishes," he replies.

Essential supplies, like medicine, water and fuel, are dwindling

They are defending their city, alongside relatives, friends, neighbours, and five brigades of pro-government troops, who can't do the job on their own.

They don't have heavy weapons or enough numbers for the expanding front line.

Training camps have sprung up for volunteers, but the lessons can be as brief as a day or two before they are sent to join the fight.

Akram (not his real name) is already a veteran. He's 31, with a child on the way.

When Taiz was fully encircled before, in 2015, he broke away from his studies and took up arms. In that round of fighting, the Houthis gained control of his district on the outskirts, and he was uprooted. He hasn't seen his old home since then.

Last month, when Taiz was completely cut off once again, he bought a rifle, paying about $1,200 (Â£910) for a Chinese version of a Kalashnikov. It used to cost less than half that sum.

He tells us this fight is different to 2015.

"This time the Houthis have new technology and new missiles," he says. "They can wage a war in the air with drones. We fear the death of our families and our civilians, and ourselves."

He has already lost four friends in the past month, who left behind wives and children.

But Akram, and many others, see defending Taiz as their duty. He says that "all the sons of Taiz" will fight the Houthis "and their extremism".

The armed group is currently designated as a foreign terrorist organisation by the US (which has gone back and forth on the issue) but not by the UK or the European Union.

"They say God gave them the right to rule everyone in Yemen. We don't accept that," says Akram. "We live in freedom. We cannot surrender; we cannot leave. We cannot breathe without our city."

Almost 350,000 people have been displaced by the fighting in recent months

But it can be suffocating inside Taiz, where nightfall brings no rest.

"Every night we have bombing and shelling," says Ahmad Al-Basha, a photographer. "I think the Houthis prefer to fight at night. Maybe they don't want us and our children to sleep inside our homes."

The people of Taiz feel let down, he tells us, by their government and by the international community. "We feel frustrated and sad," he says, "because we don't see any move to help us."

Many believe the Houthis will keep a chokehold on Taiz from the outside, and exert significant control over the city without trying to enter it.

Ahmad believes it won't come to that but fears the worst if it does.

"Maybe they will clean Taiz of any resistance, kill people and settle scores with anyone who is against them," he says.

His greatest concern is for his children, who cannot escape the sounds of war.

Like Ashwaq, the teacher, he tries to explain it away.

"I tell them it's a party," he says, "and if it's really loud, I say it's a big party."

As Taiz endures bombing, shelling, and a sense of abandonment laced with growing hunger, Ashwaq has a parent's plea for the international community.

"Please don't forget Taiz," she says. "We are ordinary families. We want our children to study, play and dream like other children around the world. We are not just numbers in news reports."

Additional reporting by Wietske Burema and Albaraa Mansoor

What Is Snoggletog? All The Tea On The Celebration At Universal Orlando This Holiday Season

It’s beginning to look a lot like Snoggletog in the Isle of Berk.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The love, joy and merriment of the holiday season are always infectious in theme parks. As part of Universal Epic Universe’s holiday celebration expansion for 2026, the decorations have been scaled up (sorry, I had to work a dragon reference in there) in How to Train Your Dragon – Isle of Berk for Snoggletog, and guests are all the better for it.

I got to experience the newborn theme park’s first holiday season in 2025, which was festive but also promised so much future possibility. Seeing what Universal was able to pull together just six months into the opening of their newest theme park just made me want more of everything. At that time, Snoggletog was relegated to a banner hung in the Isle of Berk gift shop that you could miss and never know it was there if you didn’t wander inside. So, I was particularly excited to learn that this year, the annual holiday inspired by modern Christmas traditions would be expanded throughout the land.

For those not in the know, Snoggletog is the winter holiday celebrated by the Viking community on the fictional Isle of Berk in the How to Train Your Dragon franchise. It features feasts and festivities fit for dragons and even includes the seasonal Harvest Mac & Cheese Cone at Hooligan’s Grog & Gruel. And there’s now a row of trees, just inside the entrance, decorated with what looks to be homemade ornaments that are a fun detail to check out during the day and beautiful to look at once they’re lit up at night.

Epic Universe is already a big award winner and popular destination with a ton of great attractions. I appreciate that they’re not wasting any time by expanding their offerings, too, even with something that seems as small as holiday decorations. (Not to mention there’s that construction on an Epic Universe expansion for an indoor coaster.)

While my own gothic heart remains in Darkmoor, through the Dark Universe portal, Isle of Berk feels like the most expansive and detailed area of Epic Universe, so it’s especially nice to see this holiday celebration get the extra attention it deserves. It’s true, Toothless has yet to don a Santa hat, but you never know what could be added next year and into the future.

You can check out a video overview of Snoggletog during the daytime via Orlando Informer below:

A post shared by Orlando Informer (@orlandoinformer)

I have to say, the thing that most caught my eye was the wreath full of apples. It’s the perfect decor idea that not only looks beautifully festive but would also just be delicious. I also love that you can find a tree made of wood inside Hiccup’s Workshop that you could probably easily replicate if you’re crafty, or you can just pick up themed ornaments for your own home holiday tree.

If you’re going to be in Orlando in the near future, you can experience Snoggletog at Epic Universe and then head over to the Universal Orlando Resort, where you can have a very merry Grinchmas while also rounding out your theme park trip with what is the best theme park holiday parade through January 3, 2027.

New Nvidia bug is killing SSDs — is it “AI code incompetence,” users ask?

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join the Tom's Guide Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

The latest in a string of bad days for Nvidia

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

If you haven't updated your Nvidia drivers recently, you may want to hold off. A newly discovered bug apparently can damage your PC by writing excessive amounts of data to solid-state drives (SSDs).

The bug was found in the two most recent Nvidia GPU driver updates — specifically 621.14 and 621.24 — for the Nvidia GeForce Game Ready Driver. The 621.24 update was released on November 12.

The bug was discovered by TroubleGrid who reported that the bug causes the NVDisplay.Container.exe to write (save data) far too much information to an Nvidia log file that can degrade the SSD. It can result in as much as 30GB per hour.

The executable already wrote log files before the updates but not that much. SSDs typically take years to degrade, but the excessive data written by this bug can speed up the process causing stuttering and mouse lag.

On Reddit, I've seen a few posts about the bug with users complaining that this is why you never update. "By principle I never ever update my drivers unless there are feature inclusions for let's say a new game I bought," one Redditor said.

Plenty of others agreed with that sentiment, and I'm inclined to say they're correct here.

The other half of the comments split between blaming AI-written code. Before his ouster, CEO Jensen Huang was pushing for Nvidia engineers to use more AI-generated code via chatbots and agents.

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

Or they were of the belief that Nvidia is purposely killing drives to force customers to pay more for new ones. Several called out Sam Altman and his recent claim that laptop RAM is a "terrible" allocation of a scarce resource.

"I honestly would not be surprised if they are doing this on purpose to kill our NVMe's," wrote a poster.

If we were to put blame on something, at this point, I'd say its AI-fueled incompetence, not AI-fueled greed, though that has led to said stupidity.

The easiest fix is to rollback your graphics card driver to version 621.01, which is the last known patch to lack this bug.

TroubleGrid listed a manual fix that requires you to set the Nvidia Display Container LS service to manual. Though it'll cost you. Basically, this can disable the Nvidia Control Panel and Nvidia App, meaning G-Sync color settings, display scaling, resolution overrides and more will not rock. "Do not disable the service outright if you use G-SYNC or a custom colour profile," the report warns.

If you need a temporary fix, you can stop the service, delete the log file and start again. However, the file will return and continue to grow in size.

Job posting seemingly confirms Apple is working on its own AI agent for coding and other tasks

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It is hiring a senior engineering manager to build “capable, reliable and safe” agentic tools.

Apple appears to be developing its own in-house AI agent. While we picked up on this development via Mark Gurman at Bloomberg, the news actually comes direct from the company this time. Apple has an open job listing for a senior engineering manager of agentic AI that was posted earlier this week. The description states: "As a senior leader in this org, you will own the technical direction and delivery of our agent platform and the AI capabilities built on it." It includes both general computer use and more technical coding tasks as falling within the future AI agent's purview, with the goal of being "capable, reliable and safe."

Many companies have already launched AI agents for public use this year. That includes heavy hitters in the AI space, such as Dots from OpenAI, Muse from Meta and Gemini Spark from Google. But other businesses not directly working in artificial intelligence are also embracing the agentic path.

Apple, on the other hand, has been a bit more cautious when it comes to agents. It recently warned developers that working with agentic AI "could put users at risk" by unwittingly allowing Full Disk Access. Apple then rolled out new controls earlier this week to give individuals better oversight into what data AI agents can access. Privacy is a frequent buzzword in Apple's recent software launches, so it will be interesting to see how the company walks the line whenever it unveils its own service.

Calciomercato no stop - Indiscrezioni, trattative e retroscena del 19 novembre

Commerce approved sales of more than 879,000 AI chips to Chinese companies, Rep. Mast says

📅 2026-11-19  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The subagency of the Commerce Department that regulates and enforces exports approved the sale of more than 879,000 AI chips to Chinese companies including Alibaba and Tencent as of May 2026, according to documents released Thursday by the House Foreign Affairs Committee.

Some of those approvals, made by Commerce's Bureau of Industry and Security, included sales to companies designated by the Department of Defense as "Chinese Military Companies," according to the documents. From December through May, companies had made 3.4 million requests for sales of U.S. AI chips to China, the committee reported, citing those made by Nvidia and Advanced Micro Devices.

"Over the past month, the administration's handling of chip export controls has put our national security and technology edge at risk. The Bureau of Industry and Security has failed to enforce the rules, and Congress must now conduct rigorous oversight of the administration's policies," Rep. Gregory Meeks, D-N.Y., the top Democrat on the House Foreign Affairs Committee, said at a meeting of the panel Thursday at which members voted to release the documents.

Spokespersons for Nvidia and AMD said their companies comply with U.S. export control laws.

Nvidia argued it was following the Commerce Department regulations that were in effect at the time.

A spokesperson from the Bureau of Industry and Security said it shared information with Congress on both approvals and denials of licenses for Nvidia's H200 and comparable chips. "Licensing decisions are the product of deliberation and consultation among multiple U.S. Government agencies," the spokesperson said.

"The review and licensing process spanned more than half a year, with approvals subject to strict conditions and limited volumes. Despite receiving some licenses, we have since stopped shipping H200s to China," Nvidia spokesperson John Rizzo told CNBC.

On Tuesday, Nvidia reported $2.4 billion in quarterly revenue from H200 and related chip sales in China. In total, customers based in China and Hong Kong made up about 9.5% of all revenue.

"AMD complies with applicable U.S. export control laws and regulations and maintains rigorous processes to ensure compliance across our global operations," AMD said through a spokesperson.

The approval of 879,000 AI chips is "not that many," Bernstein Senior Analyst Stacy Rasgon told CNBC, adding that this news is not surprising.

"It doesn't sound to me like they're approving everything they got willy nilly," Rasgon said of the Commerce Department. With 879,000 approvals out of 3.4 million requested AI chip sales, he added, "There was a process in place, and it sounds like they approved about one out of every four."

Despite a rapid succession of China export controls on AI chips that started under the Biden administration in 2022, Nvidia and AMD both re-engineered some chips to comply and be sold legally in China. And in December, Trump said Nvidia could ship H200s, its older generation Hopper chips, to "approved customers in China" if the U.S. got a 25% cut of sales.

The U.S. then licensed Nvidia H200 sales to Alibaba, Tencent, ByteDance and other Chinese companies, although sales were limited because Beijing didn't approve large-scale imports, chip analyst Patrick Moorhead of Moor Insights & Strategy told CNBC last week.

H200 sales numbers remained low due to those restrictions on China's end. Nvidia said H200 shipments to China made up less than 1% of data center revenue for the quarter that ended in July.

"In the grand context of the size of this market and how much they're shipping, it's a rounding error. It's tiny," Rasgon told CNBC.

The committee cited the Export Control Reform Act as justification for releasing the data. The panel said the law allows it to declassify and release export data if it "determines that the withholding of the following information is contrary to the national interest."

The license requests released by the Foreign Affairs Committee include requests to sell to major firms including ByteDance, Tesla (Shanghai) and Baidu.

The release of documents comes in the wake of Jensen Huang's resignation from the chip supplier Nvidia after news surfaced that he had several extramarital affairs, including one with a suspected Chinese spy that led to concerns about Nvidia chips being diverted to China.

In the weeks since, Republicans and Democrats in Congress have called for legislation to be passed that would impose stricter export controls on the sale of advanced AI chips to China and other adversaries.

"The license information we are disclosing today will help inform the American public about how BIS has handled licenses for Nvidia H200 and equivalent AMD chips to China. The public has the right to know how these decisions were made," Meeks said.

Mast said two of the firms listed in Nvidia license applications to BIS — Maginfra and New Matrix — are suspected "smuggling fronts." The New York Times reported in November that both companies had empty offices and had ties to companies under national security sanctions.

"Both are fake companies. If BIS approved the sale of these chips to these firms, obviously that is of tremendous concern to us — especially given that President Trump made clear last year that the sale of any AI chips to China will have very real national security conditions," Mast said at the Thursday meeting.

Maginfra and New Matrix did not reply to requests for comment.

Shares of AMD fell Thursday after the investment firm Hunterbrook Media issued a report alleging the company "has its own China problem." The report claims that "after the U.S. blacklisted Chinese server maker IEIT Systems, the company kept getting newer AMD firmware, which enables AMD chips to run in servers."

CNBC did not independently verify this information.

Fallout from the Huang scandal on Capitol Hill has intensified in the last week, as lawmakers question the degree to which the former head of Nvidia's relationship with the suspected Chinese spy could have jeopardized national security and American AI dominance.

Huang is due for a closed-door transcribed interview with the House Foreign Affairs panel and two other committees on Dec. 1. Nvidia general counsel Tim Teter is scheduled to appear before the panels Dec. 2.

Senate banking committee Chair Tim Scott, R-S.C., announced on CNBC's "Squawk Box" on Wednesday that Huang and Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick had also agreed to testify.

Lutnick has come under fire over his relationship to Huang and for his failure to take action on Megaspeed, the Singaporean company where Alice Huang, with whom the former Nvidia CEO had an affair, was an executive until 2025. U.S. investigators suspect Megaspeed diverted Nvidia chips to China.

Amid that criticism, the Commerce Department on Monday issued an interim final rule to close a loophole that has allowed Chinese companies to lease Nvidia's advancing computing power from data centers in third countries.

The rule was "written by and at the direction of" the Treasury Department and the White House National Security Council, BIS said in a press release.

— Megan Cassella, Daren Fonda and Kif Leswing contributed to this report.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/payers/consumers-believe-ai-will-be-key-making-healthcare-simpler-survey" hreflang="en">Consumers believe AI will be key in making healthcare simpler: survey</a>

📅 2026-11-19  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
Consumers believe AI will be key in making healthcare simpler: survey" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Americans want a healthcare experience that's simpler and easier to understand, and artificial intelligence may be the key to getting there, a new survey shows.

The poll, conducted by the Harris Poll and commissioned by Elevance Health, found that 82% of American patients view at least some part of healthcare information as a "foreign language," and close to half (45%) said they made a healthcare decision they later regretted due to a lack of understanding.

The survey was conducted among 2,0001 adults in mid-July, and 74% said they have encountered a key barrier, such as understanding their health insurance or a medical bill. And only a quarter (27%) said they completely understand their own health coverage.

That said, most (79%) of those surveyed said they believe AI can help to improve the healthcare experience overall.

Ratnakar Lavu, chief digital information officer at Elevance Health, told Fierce in an email that because the tech has the ability to quickly surface and parse key data about a member or health need, it can serve as a central piece of the puzzle in making a more personalized experience.

"AI-enabled tools give us the ability to create a unique member context," he said. "Because of this digital technology, we can now truly 'know the person' and proactively work with them to help them manage their healthcare needs."

For example, 82% of those surveyed said they would like to receive answers about healthcare as quickly as they can use an app to order a food delivery, and 83% would like to easily access recommendations that pull from their health coverage, individual health needs and financial situation.

Seventy-two percent said they would trust AI for accuracy on at least one type of information, such as an explanation of benefits, cost estimates or details on medication.

However, while many Americans believe AI can play a leading role in driving a better health experience, just 20% said they currently use the tech regularly as a source of information. And while 72% said they trusted the tech to surface key health information, 33% said they would trust an AI platform to translate their insurance benefits, and 30% said they trust the tech to estimate care costs.

Just 13% said they would trust AI to make diagnoses.

Lavu said that healthcare organizations looking to roll out new tools need to avoid simply embedding AI into programs for the sake of it, and be transparent about both the use case as well as the role of the human in the loop.

The study also found that women were less likely than men to trust AI, and younger generations were more likely to use it as a source of healthcare information than older groups.

"We have to implement AI at the speed of trust, which means being thoughtful about where AI adds value, not just delivering technology for technology’s sake," he said. "Trust is also earned when AI-enabled programs truly make things easier for the consumer. "

For example, Elevance Health has deployed an AI-powered program to support utilization management reviews and help drive faster approvals where possible, though AI is not used to make claims denials. It is also leaning on AI for more proactive member engagement initiatives through its digital channels, identifying individuals that would benefit from extra support or who may face gaps in care.

"We start by listening to members and identifying where healthcare is creating the most confusion or friction," Lavu said. "From there, we determine the right solution, which may or may not involve AI. When AI is the right tool, it should fit naturally into familiar interactions and make it easier for members to get an answer or take the next step."

→ Baca artikel asli

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/health-tech/hlth-2026-elsevier-upgrades-clinicalkey-ai-full-patient-ehr-context" hreflang="en">HLTH 2026: Elsevier upgrades ClinicalKey AI with full patient EHR context</a>

📅 2026-11-19  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
HLTH 2026: Elsevier upgrades ClinicalKey AI with full patient EHR context" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Elsevier unveiled this week a major upgrade to ClinicalKey AI, enabling the AI-powered clinical decision support tool to access a patient’s full electronic health record and evaluate treatment plans against the company’s evidence-based medical library.

As AI advances, Elsevier has strategically shifted from a traditional academic publisher—it’s one of the world's largest publishers of scientific, technical and medical content—to an AI-driven analytics information company, incorporating generative and agentic AI into its healthcare and academic research.

ClinicalKey AI is a HIPAA-ready point-of-care decision support tool that draws answers from full-text articles from approximately 1,000 world-leading journals. Elsevier’s upgraded ClinicalKey AI is already deployed at more than 1,200 hospitals, and the company says the solution integrates with all electronic health record providers. Elsevier recently struck a content agreement with the JAMA Network that brings full-text content and visuals from the JAMA Network Family of Journals to ClinicalKey AI.

The company has rapidly expanded its AI capabilities and features, including ClinicalKey Nursing AI, an AI-powered tool built specifically for nurses and nursing workflows. Elsevier has also developed specialized AI assistants, such as LeapSpace, an AI-assisted workspace for researchers that’s built on 15+ million peer-reviewed articles and book content and 100 million Scopus records, according to the company.

Three months ago, the company rolled out its "Ask AI" feature across more than 1,500 U.S. hospital sites using its flagship ClinicalKey platform. With the generative AI feature, clinicians can ask conversational clinical questions and receive direct, cited answers drawn from evidence-based sources, including premium journals and content from leading medical organizations. Unlike general-purpose chatbots, Ask AI does not pull from the open web and does not store, process or retain patient-identifiable information, according to the company.

In June, the company acquired Wellsheet, a health technology company that aggregates and synthesizes patient chart data—including notes, labs, imaging and medication orders—from EHRs to provide relevant clinical intelligence at the bedside. The integration of Wellsheet's patient-chart analysis enhances Elsevier’s clinical decision support with context-aware AI, according to executives.

“The problem we’re trying to solve here is that physicians are challenged by so many things when they’re taking care of patients; top among them is patient complexity. Patients have more and more conditions, and they’re taking more and more medications. It can be hard to really understand the full picture and determine how to proceed,” Dr. Claudine Lott, physician executive for clinical solutions at Elsevier, told Fierce Healthcare during the HLTH conference this week.

“Our original ClinicalKey AI tool has been a place where the clinician can go, and they can ask a question, and it searches this curated database of evidence-based content we’ve given it and gives them an answer. But there was a bit of a missing piece in that it wasn’t capturing that patient context. If you wanted to get an answer, that’s really tailored to a patient, it was limited to what you can type in,” she said.

She added, “Wellsheet has 10 years of experience with looking at the entirety of the patient chart and helping to surface that to the clinician.”

There is a growing market for AI-powered clinical decision support products, but Elsevier contends its advantage lies in its depth of patient context and its large, curated content base, with a wholly owned, large proprietary library of peer-reviewed STM literature, including content from millions of articles, journals and books.

San Juan Regional Medical Center in Farmington, New Mexico, is using ClinicalKey AI’s new patient context functionality to provide quality care with fewer resources. With smaller clinical and quality teams than large academic health systems, and in a state where nearly 40% of residents rely on Medicaid, the medical center, which serves the Four Corners area, has found this support especially valuable, according to Carlo Hallak, M.D., physician executive, information services, at San Juan Regional Medical Center.

A non-profit, community-owned acute care hospital and Level III trauma center, San Juan Regional Medical Center operates outpatient and specialty clinics alongside its main hospital campus, with associated facilities such as San Juan Regional Rehabilitation Hospital and San Juan Health Partners clinics.

Hallak emphasized that Elsevier is a primary source of medical knowledge rather than a “wrapper” for other AI platforms, and that was a key distinction for the organization, Hallak noted. And ClinicalKey AI is designed for the entire care team. “The hospital is run by nurses, mid-level providers, other caregiver teams,” he told Fierce Healthcare at the HLTH conference.

The medical center aims to move clinicians to enterprise LLMs integrated with the electronic health record that can provide the full patient context, Hallak said.

"We're already seeing how ClinicalKey AI can be instrumental at the bedside and help improve care for our patients. As a rural healthcare center, we are proud to say that by using ClinicalKey AI to check each patient’s care against the same evidence base that major health systems rely on, we’re able to deliver the standard of care patients expect in any major city or leading academic center," Hallak said.

He added, "ClinicalKey AI reads the whole chart, checks it against the evidence and shows exactly where each finding comes from. That support is invaluable for a hospital like ours, and we’re already seeing strong adoption of ClinicalKey AI among our clinicians."

Hallak cited an example of a physician who used ClinicalKey AI to correctly identify viral meningitis, preventing an aggressive and unnecessary treatment course for bacterial meningitis.

ClinicalKey AI supports the physician’s judgment rather than replacing it, helping the clinician get to relevant evidence faster, he said, noting that, in the case of the viral meningitis patient, the physician’s clinical judgment was critical in recognizing that something was unusual and then consulting colleagues. The AI tool then helped accelerate the next step by quickly surfacing information the clinician otherwise would have found by searching the medical literature.

Physicians also use the tool during weekly complex case discussions to quickly review trending medications, images and test results in one summarized sheet, he noted.

“It’s going to expedite a lot of the work they do when it comes to these complex cases,” he noted.

The AI solution can quickly summarize a complex patient history at the point of care, and this can help care teams in the emergency department understand a frequent ED patient, Hallak said.

One patient has visited their emergency department 95 times in less than a year, in addition to making numerous visits to neighboring health systems, Hallak said. When that patient arrives, clinicians who aren't familiar with him would traditionally have to dig through the chart to understand his history. With Elsevier's ClinicalKey AI tool, they can ask for a summary of the patient's recent visits and care and get that information within seconds, he noted.

Because patients who frequently visit hospital EDs typically receive care at multiple organizations, San Juan Regional Medical Center is also working with other local health systems on interoperability efforts so clinicians can get a more complete picture of what happened during the patient's visits across different facilities.

As a rural hospital with staffing constraints, technology is essential to filling workforce gaps and supporting care delivery, Hallak contends.

"We don't have enough physicians, enough nurses, enough transporters, enough caregivers. We will never be able to have enough of those," he said.

Hallak envisions ClinicalKey AI will be particularly valuable for discharging patients from the hospital faster.

Generative AI tools can help speed up patient discharges by identifying barriers, assessing patients’ care needs and surfacing relevant information from both structured and unstructured data. Because the platform is designed for the broader care team, case managers and physicians can access the same information, while push notifications can alert clinicians when test results are available so they can act quickly, Hallak said.

"From an operational perspective, it allows for this care team coordination of care, and also from a physician perspective, it provides the context whenever there's something hanging and they're trying to understand more what's happening with the patient['s] discharge. You could be a hospitalist, and you've been off shift for like seven days, and then you come to the hospital and now the patient is on their tenth day, but you inherit that discharge. Going through, reading every progress note for every single day, reading every consult for every subspecialty that looked at this patient usually would take two to three days to actually get the patient out of the building safely," he said.

Rather than manually compiling information for a discharge summary, clinicians can use ClinicalKey AI to aggregate relevant information from across the patient’s record, trace it back to its source and review the AI-generated summary before signing off on the discharge, according to Hallak.

"It is a game changer, in my opinion. One of our hospitals said it used to take me three days to discharge a patient; now it takes me less than three hours," he said.

Going forward, the system expects to measure operational outcomes such as length of stay, safe discharge and multidisciplinary-rounds use, according to Hallak. The medical center also plans to evaluate clinicians' use of the tool and how it changes care decisions.

The medical center has also deployed other AI tools such as Oracle Health's clinical AI agent for ambient documentation.

"These things are crucial. We're hoping to bring in a clinical AI agent for nurses as well to help the whole care team be able to eliminate the noise, the friction that typically EHRs or IT would bring to the conversation," Hallak said.

The medical center is taking a risk-based approach to AI governance, using source verification and peer review for AI-generated summaries while applying greater scrutiny to tools that are predictive or suggestive, Hallak noted. That oversight involves clinical experts as well as compliance, legal and cybersecurity teams along with traditional vendor security and product-vetting requirements.

AI governance should become stricter as the technology moves from summarizing information to recommending or taking actions, he said.

Elsevier designed ClinicalKey AI with security and trust in mind, including HIPAA compliance, citations that show where information comes from and safeguards that keep the tool from prescribing what clinicians should do. Instead, it surfaces relevant information to help clinicians make their own decisions, Lott said.

"Health systems tell us two things stand in the way of adopting AI: tools that don’t work with their existing EHR partners, and concerns about security, compliance and responsible use," Omry Bigger, president of Clinical Solutions at Elsevier, said. "ClinicalKey AI was designed to address both. As an EHR-supported and HIPAA-compliant ready solution, ClinicalKey AI integrates seamlessly across health systems to provide clinicians with trusted evidence and patient context that gives them tailored clinical insights for each patient at the point of care."

Diese Woche haben mir zwei Tools etwas vorgeschlagen und es direkt abgeschickt.

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Diese Woche haben mir zwei Tools etwas vorgeschlagen und es direkt abgeschickt. Das dritte hat gefragt. Ich habe nur eins davon behalten.

Der Unterschied lag nicht in der Qualität des Vorschlags, sondern im Moment davor: Vorschlag fertig – Freigabe fehlt.

FOX WIZ ist genau um diese Grenze gebaut. Die App verbindet E-Mail, Kalender, Kontakte, Aufgaben und Dateien, liest ausschließlich die Dienste, die du freigibst, und legt danach fertige Dinge bereit: eine Antwort im Postfach, einen Terminvorschlag, eine Aufgabe mit Fälligkeit. Nichts davon verlässt das Gerät in Richtung anderer Menschen, bevor du bestätigt hast. Aktionen mit Außenwirkung warten.

Spannend wird es bei der Frage, wo diese Grenze genau verläuft. Sortieren, zusammenfassen, Entwurf schreiben – geschenkt, darf laufen. Aber eine Terminzusage an drei Leute? Eine Antwort an den Vermieter? Ein Dokument, das einen Ordner wechselt, den andere sehen?

Meine Arbeitsregel bisher: Alles, was den Posteingang anderer Leute erreicht, braucht einen Menschen davor. Alles, was nur meinen eigenen Bildschirm sortiert, nicht. Das ist erstaunlich schwer sauber zu ziehen, sobald Kalender und Aufgaben mitspielen – ein verschobener Termin ist lokal, eine Einladung ist es nicht.

Wo ziehst du die Grenze? Nenne mir eine Aktion, die dein Tool ohne Rückfrage ausführen darf – und eine, bei der du sie nie erlauben würdest.
